A. THE ESTIMATE AND GAP-FILL RULE (Fred 10-20 17:15–17:18). It supersedes the earlier route → saved route → trails → straight line chain.

What was seen (Fred 10-20 last test): the lead was not heard. The radio marker did not move after the last-heard marker was dropped, even though the lead was on a track that ran on to a trail.

Fred's words: "there is a track and a trail. should we not move at least until… intersection junction?" and "our rule was use the route only if it is the only route on the current map. in lieu of the route it should be tracks and or trails." He confirmed the same rule applies to the gap fill.

CaseThe silent-lead ESTIMATE followsThe GAP FILL (between two heard points) follows
Exactly ONE route on the current mapThat route, in its direction (as built)That route
No route, or more than one routeThe track or trail the lead was on. It moves along it and stops at the next junction, never guessing a branchThe track or trail the lead was on, joined through a junction only when the next heard point is past it
Not on any track or trailHolds at the last-heard point (as now)A straight line (a polyline replaces it when one arrives)
Saved routes (not displayed)REMOVED from both. A saved route the lead's points happen to lie on is no longer used.

Status: agreed and not built. Method: desk test first, as with the trim. Replay the 10-20 Long Fast logs through the rule, then patch. The work is in TrackRules / LeadEstimate (the estimate route source, estRouteNow()) and in the gap-fill chain in TrackWriter. Callers are untouched.

Still Fred's call: whether polyline points show as dots when no route is displayed.

B. 10-21: WHAT WAS DONE ON THE NUCLEUS (node 46, V3-OS 0.11.0), through Droid 1 on its Wi-Fi, using only Nathan's tools

  • Factory config saved first, in ~/Downloads/natak46/ (factory_*.json, openapi_0.11.0.json, bridge logs). The keyed originals are private and never go on Drive or git. Scrubbed copies are on Drive GroupTrack/natak46/.
  • Radio put on GroupTrack through HIS API with nucleus_radio_grouptrack_2026-10-21.py. It does a preview, then --apply or --restore, via adb push and nc on Droid 1. It changed MEDIUM_FAST, channel GroupTrack, slot 0 and the key, through POST /api/v1/meshtastic/config/apply. The read-back matched, and the key fingerprint (psk_fingerprint = first 8 hex of SHA-256 of the key) was verified. --restore puts back his factory Natak link and SHORT_FAST.
  • Proven:
    • LoRa works both ways between node 46 and a T1000-E: 46 heard ours at SNR 11, and a text from 46 reached our rider.
    • Wi-Fi → LoRa works: a CoT from Droid 1 went out as a 52-byte ATAK packet ("TX → LoRa | GT-wifi-test-1").
    • Port 72 is NOT bridged to Wi-Fi. cot_bridge keeps only 257, text, voice and heartbeat.
  • His API:
    • Base address http://10.20.46.1/api/v1/…, no authentication.
    • 0.11.0 has no heartbeat endpoint.
    • The tx_min_interval_secs rate limit was added by Nathan on 09-24.
    • The full spec is at /openapi.json.
  • Email 1 (sent by Fred): email_to_nathan_2026-10-21_v2.eml with the proposal GroupTrack_Nucleus_RideNetwork_Proposal_2026-10-21_v9.html (artifact BTjow3KNbkrsZ7SNeaQ9Vg). It contained:
    • Request A: code (72 → CoT, uid, polyline to Wi-Fi);
    • Request B: a ~$100 bare radio;
    • Request C: S00104, the $430 scaled-down Nucleus, as Leader/Middle/Tail.
  • Security, unchanged: Nathan's default credentials, the Nucleus Wi-Fi password and the channel key never go in docs, email, memory or Drive. The channel link goes to Nathan separately.

C. 10-21: WHAT WAS DECIDED AFTERWARDS. It supersedes the proposal v9 and email 1 where they differ.

TopicDecision (Fred 10-21)Why
Message standardStay on the Meshtastic V1 standard: positions and role reports as TAKPacket on port 72, as the T1000-Es and GroupTrack already send. GroupTrack does NOT move to 257. Email 1 said it would, and that is corrected in email v3.Stock ATAK/iTAK users on any Meshtastic radio, with the plugin, read V1 on 72 directly. Port 78 (V2) exists only in 2.8, which is alpha. 257 carrying TAKPacketV2 is Nathan's own choice, and only his bridge reads it. Format and port go together: 72 = V1, 78 = V2. V1 on 78 would be discarded.
Gap polylineStays V2 on port 78, uncompressed (0xFF), GroupTrack to GroupTrack only. No polyline in ATAK/iTAK ("we do not need polylines in ATAK"). The ask to Nathan for polyline → CoT is dropped.Both ends are GroupTrack, and the radios relay any port without reading it. That is why it works today on 2.6.11. Nathan's bridge ignores 78, so ATAK never sees it.
Port 257Nathan's only. It is how his Wi-Fi riders reach LoRa (CoT → 257, as built). GroupTrack only READS 257. Nothing of ours goes on 257.Fred's "256" in an earlier message meant 257. Port 256 is Nathan's voice port.
Radio firmwareNo firmware update. The fleet stays on 2.6.11 (TAK_TRACKER → port 72).2.8.x is Alpha/Revoked only; 2.7.26 is the latest Beta. The first 2.8.0 build was revoked for T1000-E LR1110 start-up failures. #11607: TAK_TRACKER position reports stop after 10–15 min on 2.8.0, fix unconfirmed. Discussion #8724: T1000-E send/receive breaks after 2.7.15. 2.8 also defaults to LongTurbo, takes node IDs from the public key (our uid basis), turns position and telemetry off by default, and changes nRF52 storage (full erase). A bench test on Billy beans waits for a 2.8 Beta.
The NucleusPaused. Starlink and the internet link are removed (off grid). Ten bare radios do everything ten Nucleus units do for GroupTrack, and for ATAK/iTAK users with a radio. The Pi adds only Wi-Fi for radio-less ATAK users near its cart, the internet link, and voice. S00104 at $430 is not justified per cart; the bare radio at ~$100 (+$50 optional power) is the ask.All cart-to-cart work is LoRa. Nucleus Wi-Fi range does not cover a spread-out group. His default SHORT_FAST is shorter range than our MEDIUM_FAST. Range comes from the radio (the 1 W module plus external antenna), not the Pi.
Wi-Fi (IP) instead of BluetoothBluetooth stays. Wi-Fi is not shown to be more reliable. ESP32-class bare radio requested only as an option.Meshtastic documentation:
  • "Enabling Wi-Fi will disable Bluetooth" (one or the other);
  • "SoftAP mode is not supported" (the radio cannot be the hotspot);
  • reports of ESP32 Wi-Fi drops after 1–2 h, about 20% loss (old 1.3 firmware);
  • TCP is the secondary transport on Android.
An Android hotspot without a SIM or internet varies by device (missing on Wi-Fi-only tablets). A local-only hotspot gets a random name and password each start, which a radio cannot store. Check: Droid 1 and Droid 2 in airplane mode with Wi-Fi on → does Hotspot switch on? If a fixed network is ever needed, a USB travel router costs far less than a Pi.
Starlink later?If an off-ride view is ever wanted, the lead's GroupTrack tablet is the gateway: the mesh by radio plus the internet by Starlink Wi-Fi, forwarding to a TAK server or AWS. No Pi.Every cart's position reaches the lead over LoRa.

    3. voice airtime on LoRa;
    4. what a Nucleus adds when every cart has a radio;

  • the bare-radio ask: 1 W LoRa, external antenna, GPS, Bluetooth, USB power, strap or fixed mount, proven at 1 mile on MEDIUM_FAST against a T1000-E, ~$100 + $50 power option, ESP32-class optional;
  • S00104 not justified unless his answers change the picture.

E. 2.7b APPLICATION SIMPLIFICATION: remove screens, let the processes take their parts as input (Fred 10-18 direction; tasks set out 10-21 12:01)

Fred 10-21: remove screens, "letting functions take parts as input so screens are not called back to back", "moving data to the launch screen", and "running processes without further screen prompts".

Fred 10-18: the app is overwhelmingly complex, and navigation must be simplified. Take the screens out of processes and move their value forward to the processes that call them, so we are not nesting six layers deep. Each flow executes one task. Separate the prompt screen from a parameter-driven process. Simplification follows the tick removal ("we will have tamed the app"), and the tick is now gone.

The rule each flow must meet:

  • ONE launch screen collects everything the task needs, with defaults pre-filled from the ride or settings.
  • It hands those values to a process function as parameters.
  • The process runs to the end with no further screen prompts.
  • A process never opens another screen to get a value; that value is a parameter or a default.
  • The only allowed stop is a confirmation before something destructive or irreversible.
  • No screen-to-screen chains.
TaskWhatHow / output
S1 InventoryEvery function that shows a screen, panel or dialog, and who calls it. Find every chain where a screen or process opens another screen, with its depth (the "six layers").The function references (function universe / where-used, the navxref tool) → a table: flow, launch screen, chain of screens, depth, the values each intermediate screen collects. Desk work only, no code change. Comes first.
S2 Split prompt from processFor each chain, pull the work out of the screens into a process function that takes its parts (parameters) as input.Process functions callable with no UI: testable, and reusable from a check-in, a ride package or another process.
S3 Move the data forwardThe values the intermediate screens collected move to the launch screen, or to defaults from the ride or settings. The intermediate screens are removed.One launch screen per task. Fewer screens in total.
S4 Order of workThe deepest and most-used chains first. One flow per patch, bench-tested before the next (as with the tick removal).The S1 table ranked by depth × use.
S5 ReadabilityThe text navigation line (Search, Map Features, Map Keys, Group Help, GRP Awareness, Work with Rides) is hard to read over some maps. The colour choices on the artifact detail panels.Contrast that holds on every basemap (satellite, red rock, topo).
S6 Side menuOne button slides a panel out with the navigation options, and the panel closes once an option is chosen. It replaces the text line.Fred's idea (10-18 19:37).
S7 Compare onX and GaiaHow they keep navigation flat.A short comparison to guide S3 and S6.

Also still open at the back of 2.7b: the radio-setup timeout on a delayed reboot (Wio Tracker). It reads like the phantom connection that spoils the reconnect.

The gap polyline (POLYLINE-2026-10-20) -- the design as built

  • Lead side (GroupTrack): dead air = the time the LEAD hears NO other cart (one cart heard = the hops relay to the rest). After ≥ 60 s of it (Fred 14:45: "a good even number" = six broadcasts), the first report heard from another cart sends ONE polyline: 20 points at equal time steps across [last heard .. heard again] from the lead's own radio fixes (option 1), plus one repeat at the next report heard ≥ 10 s later. Fewer than 2 own fixes in the window = nothing sent (logged). The lead = checked in as Leader, or the cart locked as lead at REC. Pop-up "Gap polyline sent: m:ss of dead air".
  • Receive, three layers (Fred 14:27 -- keep GroupTrack's message out of the mesh layer): core/data TakShapeInbox (NEW, generic: ANY TAK drawn shape, no GroupTrack knowledge; POLYTEST's GtPolylineInbox retired -- renamed *.removed_POLYLINE-2026-10-20, uncommitted, delete at clean-up) → comm layer MeshCommMessaging recognises u-d-f + uid GT-gap- → CommEvent.PolylineReceived (NEW, additive) → GroupTrack onGapPolylineReceived: pop-up; the LEAD's polyline with REC on → TrackEntry.Poly → LeadCartProcess.onGapPolyline → TrackWriter.pin. The repeat is recognised (sender + start) and not applied twice.
  • Pin (Fred 14:02-14:04, 14:47): the polyline REPLACES -- never a second line. Kept: the lead track up to the LAST REAL point heard before the window; removed: everything inferred after it; the 20 points joined in order by the fill rules (displayed route → known tracks → saved routes → trails → straight); the reconnect point joined to the first real point after the window; everything after stays. A point more than 50 m off the displayed route (or off every known line near it) is a DOT, never joined ("one point off turns the path into angles").
  • Radio setup: rebroadcast mode = ALL is now managed at check-in (written + verified: 25 checks); NONE / CORE_PORTNUMS_ONLY would stop relaying or drop TAK.
  • Desk test: LF loop 10,845 m pinned vs 10,768 m real (4 dots); afternoon 10,954 vs 11,115 (2 dots).
  • Road test 15:30-15:47 (Droid 1 leading the loop, Droid 2 parked): Droid 2 last heard the lead 15:29:01; the lead's own dead air 15:30:19 → 15:36:18 = 359 s; sent 15:36:18 (20 points, 202 bytes), received 2.1 s later (the clock difference within that), pinned with 10 of 20 as dots; repeat 15:36:38 recognised. All 20 points lie on the road Droid 1 drove (19 within 0-2 m, 1 at 22 m between fixes). The 10 dots = where this afternoon's loop left this morning's saved tracks (7 points 66-168 m from them; no route displayed) -- the rule worked as written; proposal (Fred to decide): with NO route displayed, a polyline point joins straight (it is the lead's real position).

The trim (TRIM-2026-10-20) -- my cart replaces the lead track behind me

  • Fred 14:49-14:56: "rather than overlay it replaces"; "any lead cart point behind my cart ... trimmed at the next my-track write"; BY TIME, not geometry ("we had problems with these rules in twists"); all times UTC epoch ms (Arizona → Utah is a time-zone change -- local time would wreak havoc).
  • Each MY-CART position (the feed that moves my icon -- tablet GPS, or my radio without a chip): the first lead-track SEGMENT within 50 m of my cart, searching FORWARD ONLY from the last cut; on to the next segment only while my cart is level with the joint (twists); DRIVING ORDER -- the cut moves no further along the lead's line than my cart has driven since the last cut (+150 m; steps ≥ 5 m, jitter is not driving). The DISPLAY is cut at that segment's start, by time; the written lead track stays whole (fills, pins, other carts' off-track). Polyline pins are trimmed the same way.
  • Desk test (TrimSim, Droid 1's real fixes lf / est / range, follower 30 s .. 20 min behind, my-cart updates every 3 / 6 / 10 s -- 45 runs): never skipped ground; the cut at most ~60 m ahead of my cart (where the lead was parked); at most one lead segment behind (≤ 435 m on these fixes; shorter with route fill). Two failures found and fixed on the way: point matching jumped 10 km to an out-and-back's return leg (fixes ~160 m apart at speed) → segments; a parked follower was cut to the lead's return home after a 10 km loop → driving order. A chipless tablet (6 s radio fixes) trims the same (Fred's concern, 16:04).
  • Road: Fred 15:47 "the trim definitely worked"; the log cut repeatedly from 15:42 (36 → 33 segments and on).

Range and the estimate (morning field tests -- Droid 2 parked at home, Droid 1 driving the loop)

  • Fred's worry: the 200-yard range of 10-19. Medium Fast loop, Long Fast loop, the estimate loop. Reliable range ~470-550 yd in dense housing (NH granite ledges); loss and regain symmetric within ~1 min. Long Fast adds only 10-30% → Medium Fast stays the default. Real Utah rides keep carts at most about 1 mile apart. Two MOVING carts are needed for an adequate test.
  • Estimate error at reconnect on highway-speed gaps: ride-average speed 1.9-2.6 km. The source track's time profile measured ~200-450 m but Fred rejected it ("highway speeds, not real trail").
  • A 2-minute polyline lifts known coverage of a gap from ~10% to ~50%, a 10-minute one to 100% (analysis that led to sending the TOTAL dead air).

Decisions (Fred, 10-20)

  • Silent lead: blink and project along the route; no track drawn for the projection; corrected when heard; after 2 missed broadcasts; speed = the ride's average over moving segments; the error in the statistics; shown in group mode (auto-pan includes it).
  • Polyline: the lead's TOTAL dead air; 60 s minimum; 20 equal-time points; sent when the lead hears normal reports from other carts (not every 30 s) + one repeat; a standard TAK format (CoT u-d-f) -- "cobble together a set message sent on demand and test send/receive before we go crazy"; receivers correct the existing lead track (replace, not a second line); > 50 m off the route = dots; rebroadcast ALL in the check-in settings; GroupTrack's message kept out of the mesh layer (three layers).

Rejected today -- and why

  • The source track's time profile for the estimate -- highway speeds, not real trail riding (Fred).
  • Sending the polyline every 30 s -- send when the lead hears other carts again; a polyline sent into dead air reaches nobody.
  • Drawing the polyline as a second line -- it must correct the lead track in place.
  • A 2-minute polyline -- covers ~50% of a 10-minute gap; the total dead air covers all of it.
  • GtPolylineInbox in core/data -- GroupTrack knowledge in the mesh layer (test only; replaced by the generic TakShapeInbox).
  • Trim by geometry -- failed in twists before; trim by time. Point matching -- jumped to an out-and-back's return leg (segments instead). Unlimited forward search -- a parked follower skipped a whole loop (driving order).
  • Orange lead track -- invisible on red rock; a dashed lead track -- the path ahead must be the strong line.

Nucleus -- what Nathan sent (16:18) and what the code says (V3_OS 0.16.0, 10-18)

  • Fred has nodes 46 and 47: each raises its own Wi-Fi AP when powered (the name carries 0046 / 0047); join with Nathan's default credentials (never written in docs or chat); web UI at 0046-nucleus.local / 0047-nucleus.local (Wi-Fi or Ethernet on the home network); V3-OS; the LoRa bridge is ON by default, listening on the ATAK default multicast ports; the radios are on Short Fast (changeable in the Meshtastic web UI, Radio configuration); either USB port for the Wi-Fi radio. Nathan is building mounts and a Reticulum/HaLow module.
  • From the code (cot_bridge.py): the bridge carries ONLY port 257 (compressed TAKPacketV2) between LoRa and multicast 239.2.3.1:6969 (+ text port 1, voice, heartbeat) -- if portnum != "ATAK_FORWARDER": return. Our T1000-E positions (port 72) and today's polyline (port 78) are NOT carried to Wi-Fi. Asked of Nathan 09-25 for 72; not in. Decide: Nathan carries 72 + 78, or GroupTrack sends on 257 when a Nucleus rides.
  • Its radio: Short Fast, channel "Natak" -- ours: Medium Fast, channel GroupTrack -- they will not hear each other until matched. API /api/v1/meshtastic/config/apply sets owner, region, preset, role, hop limit, TX power, frequency slot, channel name, PSK (async: read → apply changes → poll op-status → read) -- the same shape as our check-in. No login on the API (access = being on the network) -- the 09-24 security-layer question stands. meshtasticd on localhost:4403; TX_MIN_INTERVAL default 30 s (a setting).
  • The test Fred set (16:23): a T1000-E on the same mesh network as the Nucleus; Droid 1 joins the Nucleus Wi-Fi instead of Bluetooth. What has to be true: the T1000-E and the Nucleus radio share preset + channel + key + frequency slot; the bridge carries the T1000-E's position (port 72 today is NOT carried); GroupTrack reads CoT from the Wi-Fi (UDP multicast 239.2.3.1:6969) -- or connects to the Nucleus's meshtasticd over TCP (localhost:4403 on the box; whether it is reachable from the Wi-Fi is to check) -- the comm layer's IP implementation.
  • Step 1 for 10-21 (read-only): power node 46, join its AP, open the web UI, then for p in version status meshtastic/status meshtastic/config meshtastic/nodes; do echo "== $p"; curl -s -m 5 http://0046-nucleus.local/api/v1/$p; echo; done.

Lessons (keep)

  • A desk replay on real fixes found both trim failures before a tablet saw them -- the model first, every time.
  • Measure the right thing: the "200-525 m ahead" was the measurement counting the whole segment, not the trim.
  • Check a reported problem against older logs first: the foreground-service error predates today's work.
  • A test message on demand proved the radio path (both ways, points exact) before the feature was built on it.
  • Quotes inside generated patch text broke a patch file once today (14:56) -- syntax-check every patch file before it reaches Downloads.

What the logs and the code proved today (evidence, not theory)

  • The tablet GPS chip needs about 5 minutes of open sky for its first lock (Unisoc chip; no SIM, no Wi-Fi assist). Log: fixStatus=A/V, num_svs. Recording works once it has locked (13:20 run). Not a defective chip (my wrong claim this morning, withdrawn).
  • The second stream of my cart on other tablets was our own role report (every 30 s, it carried my cart's position = the tablet's since ONEFEED). Fred: "check the logs" -- they showed it. Fixed by ROLEPOS.
  • Sleep 84 s after REC (Droid 2): REC at 11:11:50 in the same app process as the 10:48 recording → the sleep clock still held the earlier time → "no movement for 25 min" at the first points (11:13:14). A recording that ended asleep also left every new point dropped. Fixed by RECSLEEP.
  • The z0 / white screen: every restart that restored the saved frame was followed ~2.5 s later by a zero-size view at 43.06888777, -71.71875 -- exactly my cart rounded to one pixel at ZOOM 0 -- then the whole world. Leaflet's fitBounds works the zoom out from the map's size; called before the map has a size it picks zoom 0. setView (given its zoom) never did this. Offline there are no z0 tiles = white. The saved frames themselves were good (my first theory, a 0,0,0,0 saved frame, was wrong). Only ONE place restores the ride map from the saved file (start-up, ConvoyScreen ~964 + drawPersistedState); FIT uses the database's own bounds. Fixed by FITSIZE + MAPSTARTUP.
  • The lead track did not follow home6 on the 13:20 drive because Fred drove it against its arrows and the forward-only rule refused the route (simulation lead_sim_track13_2026-10-19_v2.png). Fixed by ROUTEBOTH.
  • This morning's Droid 1 Bluetooth stack was stuck: registerApp got no onClientRegistered, scans failed with ApplicationRegistrationFailed; only Bluetooth off/on cleared it. FORGET does remove the radio from Android when it is really connected (Fred).
  • Droid 1's radio GPS mode was found DISABLED (Fred 17:14) -- its position had been frozen at 42.80064,-71.27742 since 09:55. GroupTrack's default writes gpsMode: ENABLED and the setup checks it, so it was not our setup. Likely (not certain): the Meshtastic firmware toggles GPS on a triple press of the user button -- the T1000-E's only button, pressed all day for power cycles. After both radios were powered down and reconnected both tablets saw each other (17:28); that radio still had a weak GPS signal. "1 radio, 0 active" in GRP Awareness = radio linked but sending nothing.
  • The reconnect panel v3 never opened (Droid 1, ~17:00): it was gated on "riding with a group" = this tablet sees at least one other cart -- false, because Droid 1 heard no other cart. The log was lost to a reboot; the cause is from the code and what Fred saw. v4 gates on group awareness ON.
  • A reboot wipes the log buffer AND resets logcat -G 16M (twice today). Pull logs before any reboot; set -G again after one.
  • Android 13+ will not let an app switch Bluetooth (we target 36): the panel uses Android's own dialogs. The turn-OFF dialog (android.bluetooth.adapter.action.REQUEST_DISABLE) is not a published Android call -- whether these tablets show it is a tablet test (fallback: Android's Bluetooth screen after 8 s).
  • Recovery timing is inherited, not measured: tries 45 s apart and "stuck connecting" 45 s came from LINKHB (10-05). A normal reconnect took ~20-25 s in the setup logs; once 98 s after an install.
  • Range this morning: Droid 2 lost between about 100 and 175 m out. Droid 2's clock is Utah time (2 h behind).

Sleep is the deliberate disconnect -- it must not open the panel.

Lessons (keep)

  • Check the logs before theorising -- today's sleep, z0, second stream and GPS-mode problems each looked deep and were one small thing found from evidence. Where I guessed first (defective chip, Wi-Fi, upstream location sharing, a 0,0,0,0 saved frame) I was wrong.
  • Kotlin builds a class top to bottom, and a local function must be declared before it is used -- two of my patch errors today (the C1 listener above init; v2's ON effect above its fallback). Check declaration order in every patch.
  • Pull the log before a reboot; a reboot also resets logcat -G.
  • Never a patch on a patch -- each panel version undid the last (git checkout) and was written whole.
  • zsh: quote --include='*.kt'; Droid 2 shows Utah time.

7. Lessons

  • Don't guess route names — copy what works: four Hide Mesh patches named destinations that were not on the back stack; the fix was the existing Settings mechanism.
  • Read the counts before naming a cause: the slow-map theory was wrong — TICKSHADOW showed 0–2 updates per 5 s; wpa_supplicant showed the Wi-Fi.
  • I invented a NO LEAD ASSIGNED message nobody asked for — Fred rejected it. Build what was decided.
  • We moved where my cart's positions come from but not its message updates (Fred) — a source change must follow the data all the way to the output.
  • Test on the road, not the bench: the my-cart freeze only showed riding with the radio on.
  • zsh: no word starting with =, no ! in double quotes, quote grep patterns with ( — single quotes everywhere.

What the logs proved today (evidence, not theory)

  1. No GPS fix was treated as a dead radio. LINKHB (10-05) made "talking" = my radio's own TAK report; a T1000-E sends that only with a fix. The REAL healthStep code run in a simulation (scratchpad hs18): a 5-min fix gap = 4 forced reconnects (before), 2 (HEALTHTRAFFIC, alone on the road), 0 (GPSNOTFATAL). Each forced reconnect is a Bluetooth teardown — the wedge of 10-16 / 10-18 morning.
  2. The phantom link is a Meshtastic fault. 14:04:32 Android reported the radio ending the connection (BluetoothGatt status=19); the app kept link=CONNECTED until our silence rule forced a reconnect. SILENCE30S now catches it: 30 s message, 45 s reconnect.
  3. Meshtastic's Bluetooth keep-alive is empty. AndroidRadioInterfaceService.keepAlive() sends a heartbeat every 30 s only over USB; NordicBleInterface.keepAlive() only logs. Nothing checks a Bluetooth radio. BLEHEARTBEAT patch written (not applied).
  4. Reconnect attempts stack up. When Billy beans came back (14:20:15) there were 8 configureMTU calls at once — one per earlier try. No harm proven; no fix yet.
  5. Billy beans sent no position 14:02–14:26 — neither tablet heard it; it was relaying fred&June to Droid 2 the whole time. Its first broadcast came 2 s after Fred's second manual reconnect (14:26:06 → 14:26:08): cause or coincidence UNKNOWN. A first manual reconnect at 14:24 was NOT followed by broadcasts.
  6. COTMSG is rate-limited by design (one log line per radio per minute, MeshCommMessaging.kt line 128); every received report becomes a CoT message on the board.
  7. The check-in lead works: Droid 2 received fred&June's TeamLead (15:32:05, 16:38:26) → LEADBOARD lead = !23dec46a.
  8. The lead track trim fails on a loop. LeadTrack.shownSegments cuts at the LAST lead point within 50 m of my cart; when the lead comes back to where I am, his newest point is beside me and the whole loop is hidden (Droid 2: 1→7 segments, then 0 at 15:35:14 / 16:41:05; Fred saw ~100 ft). OPEN.
  9. No GPX / blue line on Droid 1 in group mode was a 2.7b regression (10-15). 2.7a started the tablet chip every tick (cartFromMember → if (!shadow) startPhoneGps()). When the board took over (shadow tick) the only start left was TICKSMALL, gated on !radioTalkingNow — with the radio connected nothing ran the chip until REC; REC started a cold chip; with no internet its first fix took 4 min 46 s (16:37:51 → 16:42:37), after the 3-min loop. CHIPALWAYS: chip fixes from the moment of REC (17:20:53, 2.5 m), 39 GPX points.
  10. Logs: the Mac's capture runs over USB and stops at unplug; Droid 1's own log memory drops the app's lines first. Before every drive: adb -s <serial> logcat -G 16M; after: adb -s <serial> logcat -d -v threadtime > file. LEAD SET / TRACK START go to the app's convoy_debug.log, never to logcat.
  11. Smart broadcast: a stationary radio reports about every 20 s.

The track rewrite — written once, with the route/trail hierarchy designed in (Fred 10-18 07:36–08:06). Cart positions (other carts and my cart) are already off the tick (board, received CoT messages; OWNTAKPOS makes my radio's own position readable). The tick's drawing of the lead track goes with the tick, and my track has never been drawn live — so both tracks are written new, as isolated callable modules, with placeholders for the parts that come in later builds.

ModuleWhat it doesBuild 1Later
TrackPath (plain Kotlin, no Android — testable alone)previous point + new point + map context (loaded route, nearby trails) → the points to draw between them, the snapped location of the new point, and the rule used: ROUTE · TRAIL · STRAIGHT · WAIT.
Order: ROUTE (both points within 50 m of the loaded route → path along the route) → TRAIL (nearest trail within the snap distance → path along it; at a junction WAIT until the next point BEYOND the junction is received — never guess a branch) → STRAIGHT.		 STRAIGHT works; ROUTE and TRAIL are placeholders that answer "not applicable" and log itbuild 3 ROUTE filled · build 4 TRAIL + junction wait — inside the module only, callers untouched
CartMotiona received message → the path from TrackPath split into N one-second steps; the cart icon (and the drawn track) advance each second so carts move steadily. Goal (Fred 07:58): the mapping progress COMPLETES BEFORE THE NEXT UPDATE arrives; after a long silence (e.g. 2 min) there will be a jump — accepted. placeholder = the full gap in ONE step (Fred 08:06: today's behaviour)build 5 stepping

CartMotion rules (proposed 07:56, taken as agreed 07:58): the steps are display-only — not stored as track points, never sent, no last-heard or status change, not used for off-track or the 50 m passed rule (only real reports count); catch-up from the last real point to the new one, never projected ahead (consistent with "wait for real fixes"); N = the cart's NORMAL report interval so the steps finish before the next expected report (observed radios ~20 s, configured 6 s / 30 s); a report that arrives early continues from wherever the icon is. The stepping is a short job per received message, not a standing timer. Separate NON-software task: maximise cart broadcast distance to shrink the dead spots (preset MEDIUM_FAST vs LONG_FAST, antenna placement).

The lead track (Fred 10-17 22:27–10-18 07:44):

  • Built — the same on every tablet: from the LEAD CART's own positions only; when he is quiet it ends at his last known position — no stand-ins (the proxy snap stays killed). ONE lead track whoever leads: old points stay as assigned, new points come only from the current leader, a lead change CONTINUES the track (replaces "a lead change starts a new line"). Every real point kept.
  • Lead new position = the route/trail location of the point (TrackPath's snapped location) + the track line to it (Fred 08:06).
  • Signal Drop: the track WAITS for a real fix (no projected points); then the gap last-known → current is drawn over the route if loaded, else over the trail(s), else straight (TrackPath).
  • Shown — per tablet: the purpose is the lead's path AHEAD of me, to his last point. Lead-track points my cart has PASSED are removed from the display on my tablet (kept in the data): PASSED = my cart within 50 m of the point; that point and every earlier one hidden. My cart is always drawn over the track.
  • Off-track: unchanged — OFF_TRACK_MILES from Settings, measured when THAT cart's own position arrives.
  • T7 (after the tick): the lead cart's position = the LAST POINT of the lead's polyline; the 2-minute polyline replaces the tail broadcast.

My cart and my track:

  • My cart (#16 closed, Fred 08:00–08:02): no special case. The tablet's GPS fix already enters the board as a message (MYCARTMSG), so it follows the same TrackPath + CartMotion rules as every cart. When my radio drops, the tablet's messages keep coming — no break; with no tablet GPS, my radio's messages take over.
  • My track: drawn live behind my cart from the recorder's points (ConvoyGpsService 3000 ms / 5 m — dense, so STRAIGHT, no snap, no junction wait); the points stay on the tablet, never broadcast. On a tablet with NO GPS, my track is fed by my radio's fixes (~20 s) through TrackPath like the lead track. A 2-second recorder interval goes after the tick (Claude's call 07:47: the recorder writes every saved track — file size, battery, rider-trail matching).

Board clock (must precede T5.5): today the board ages statuses (Active → Signal Drop → Lost) only when a message arrives — proved (B3: Droid 1 10-16 08:22 tick DROP vs board ACTIVE). The tick is what still ages them on rollback; removing it without a replacement leaves a silent cart Active for ever. A minimal clock — ageing only — keeps a behaviour the tick provides; it is not an addition.

Time (Fred 10-18 08:08 → 08:09): times are a DURATION from ride initiation (REC pressed) — elapsed time is meaningful and consistent across tablets and time zones. Underneath, every message carries its UTC epoch time; elapsed = message epoch − ride-start epoch. TrackPath / CartMotion / the board never use a tablet's local clock time (Droid 2 runs on Utah time, so its logs read 2 h behind; its epoch time is the same). Ride start = each tablet's own REC press (Fred 08:11). RECEIVED-ACTIVITY RECORD (Fred 08:14 → 08:16): each tablet writes a detail file of the activity it RECEIVES — for every received report of every cart: the cart, the time received, the elapsed time since this tablet's REC, the message's own time, the location (and speed / course when sent). It is the source for the metrics later and for CartMotion's steady segments (each cart's normal report interval). Right now it only RECORDS the data — no broadcast change, no calculations (distance, metrics) yet. Format (Fred 08:17–08:20): a separate ride detail JSON beside the track file (<track>_ride_detail.json), held in memory by cart, written at END and when the app goes to the background (event, not timer). It holds RECEIVED reports only — my tablet's own GPS points are already in the track file and are not doubled. Lead (Fred 08:22): the lead is identified in the JSON — each report carries lead: true when that cart was the lead at that moment, and a leadChanges list records every change (elapsed, from, to) — so a replay can draw the lead track, then my cart's track. Per-cart device data (Fred 08:24): each cart's entry also records its radio's hardware model (e.g. T1000-E — from the radio's node record), team and role, and for each received report the distance from this tablet's position to the sender and the signal (SNR / RSSI) — so weak devices can be found and routed out, and real broadcast/receive distances worked out per radio type. Placement: the ride detail recording, the replay and the metrics are part of RIDE METRIC REPORTING — after the tick (Fred 08:22). After the tick: a ride REPLAY at selectable speeds showing every cart's movement over the trail from each cart's perspective (each tablet's file = its view).

Ride detail JSON — the target definition (v1, Fred 10-18 08:14–08:26)

What it is: each tablet's record of the messaging payload it RECEIVED during one recording (REC → END) — the full received-message data captured for later analytics (ride metrics, replay at selectable speeds, CartMotion's per-cart report interval, weak-device and range analysis). Built AFTER the tick, as part of ride metric reporting; this definition is the target. File: <track name>_ride_detail.json beside the recording's track file (separate — the track file keeps my tablet's own GPS points; they are not doubled here). Writes: held in memory, grouped by cart; written at END and when the app goes to the background (an event, not a timer). Time: every time is UTC epoch ms; e = elapsed seconds since THIS tablet's REC (ride start = each tablet's own REC press).

Pipeline (Fred 08:30–08:31): 1) receive — the comm side makes the CoT message; the board updates the cart's TRUTH at once (last heard, status, lead) and the ride detail JSON records the report (when built — on RECEIVE, never on process); lead-track points and off-track come from the real report here. 2) CartMotion black box → 3) map position function → one or more queue entries (cart, position, due time, real / in-between) → 4) map queue: every radio receipt goes through it; an entry is processed when due — cart icon moves, the drawn track extends through TrackPath, the 50 m trim applies. In-between steps are display-only and never recorded. One queue, one process (Fred 08:33–08:34): every received message goes into the RECEIVING QUEUE (stamped with its received time); one consumer takes them in order, updates the board truth, writes the ride JSON (placeholder until the metric reporting), and CartMotion puts N entries back on the SAME queue — N = 1 now, N = the cart's steps later; same queue, same process, only N changes. Each entry is flagged real or in-between: only the REAL one updates the truth and the JSON; in-between entries only move the display. First pass (build 1): the FULL message only — ONE entry per received message, due now; no per-second entries driven by the report frequency (later, build 5).

Lead change (Fred 08:39–08:41): the new lead is picked up from where he is; NO line is drawn for him until he passes the last known lead position — he comes within 50 m of it, and the track continues from his NEXT report (ahead) — no backwards lines. Lead silent = no lead movement until heard again; heard again = the track is laid from his last point to the new one (over route/trail) on every map, shown back to each tablet's own cart. My track trails my cart.

Queue placement (08:37): the receiving queue + its consumer run in the BACKGROUND, ONCE for the app (an app-wide owner — not one per view-model copy, item J, or every message would be processed and written twice); the map drawing runs in the FOREGROUND, only while the map shows (hidden: keep each cart's latest position, catch up on return).

Model run (tracksim_2026-10-18.py, 10-18 08:36–08:45): 15 min, reports every 20 s, lead silent 140 s, a lead change at 10:00 to a cart 200 m behind — at N = 1 and at N = steps: no lead points during the silence (status Drop) · JSON = real messages only · lead changes logged · only points ahead of my cart shown · NO backward lines. Rules the model forced (now part of the design): (1) a new real report CANCELS that cart's pending steps; (2) N = the NORMAL interval (median of the last 5 gaps), never the last gap — a 160 s gap had produced 160 overlapping steps; (3) the lead LINE grows from its own end toward the real point, not from the cart icon; (4) the JSON leaves out my tablet's own GPS points; (5) the 50 m trim uses the real lead points.

THE PROCESS — seven boxes, one queue (Fred 08:45; model process_model_2026-10-18.py): 1 Receiver (every report → the receiving queue, stamped; background, once for the app) → 2 Truth (last heard, position, lead + the CLOCK, ageing only) → 3 Recorder (ride JSON on receive; not my tablet's GPS) → 4 LeadTrack (real lead points, one track, lead-change hold, waits through silence, off-track per report) → 5 TrackPath (ROUTE / TRAIL placeholders, STRAIGHT) → 6 Segmenter (CartMotion: N entries on the same queue; N = 1 now; a new report cancels pending entries) → 7 Map (foreground: icon moves, lead line grows from its end, 50 m trim, my track behind my cart).
Tests 10-18 08:50 — one message: processed, queue empty, line started, 1 JSON report. Three carts with connects/disconnects for each (L silent 140 s, B gone 350 s, my radio off 60 s) and a lead change at 10:00, at N = 1 and at N = steps: my cart from the tablet during my radio drop, Active, my track without gaps · L Drop while silent, NO lead points added · B Lost while gone, Active on its first report back · 0 backward lines · JSON 92 real reports, none of my tablet's GPS · lead changes logged · shown line only ahead of my cart · queue drains. The gap after L's silence = one 1,280 m straight segment at N = 1 (≤ 64 m steps at N = steps) — ROUTE fills it along the route later.

Found on the 10-18 walk: Droid 2 (no GPS chip) gets rough NETWORK fixes at home; MYCARTMSG's rule (any tablet fix in 15 s overrides the radio) threw its radio's real GPS away, and the RADIOTRACK recorder feed sits after that return — no blue track on a no-GPS tablet. This replaces the 08:00 reading of #16 (tablet first).

NO GPS IS NOT A FATAL ERROR (Fred 10-18 11:50–11:51): "treating it as one hoses the Bluetooth connection because of our recovery". Found today: a radio with no fix (trailhead, restart indoors) broadcasts nothing in TAK_TRACKER; the health check read "no own report" as "radio dead", forced reconnects on a working link (10:06–10:32: Droid 1 heard throughout), gave up, cleared the other carts and wedged Android Bluetooth (resets on both Droids). Agreed design: (1) health = the LINK, not GPS — HEALTHTRAFFIC now, a ping later; (2) messages that say what is wrong — yellow "Radio connected: waiting for a GPS fix — other carts can't see you yet" vs red "Radio not answering — reconnecting…"; "forget and re-pair" only when Android says unpaired; (3) recovery NEVER gives up while riding — reconnect to the LAST radio, fast then every 1–2 min, never store "n", never clear the other carts for a GPS gap; (4) my cart stays on my map from the tablet's GPS meanwhile; (5) to verify on a radio: can the tablet hand its GPS position to the radio while the radio has no fix (others then see you).

9. Lead track · my cart and my track · lead polyline · removing the tick — the design in full

Carried here in full (Fred 10-15 EOD: the living docs must hold it, not point to the plan). Source: Tick Replacement Plan v6, sections 0, 4c, 5, 6, 11, 12, 14, 17.

A. The lead track — what the code does today (in the tick, ConvoyViewModel ~1172–1293):

  • While REC is on, each NEW lead position (the lead's own report, never a substitute) adds one straight segment from the previous lead position to the new one (_routeTrailSegments).
  • A lead change starts a new line. _leadTrackSegments = the lead only, drawn black (or per-cart colours).
  • Off-track = a cart farther than OFF_TRACK_MILES (Settings → Off Track) from any segment, swept every tick.
  • Proxy snap (a cart within ~250 ft of the quiet lead's last position stands in for the lead) — KILLED (Fred 15:15).
  • Found 10-15 on the ride: the lead's OWN tablet drew no lead track — its lead position came from its own radio's record, which never moved on its own tablet (the same cause as the my-cart freeze; MYCARTMSG fixes the source).

B. The lead-track rules (Fred 10-15 13:25–13:29):

  • Source order: 1) the route if loaded — a lead position within 50 m is snapped onto the route and the track runs along the route (ROUTETRACK); 2) no route → the trails — snap to the nearest trail and follow it; 3) neither nearby → the raw reported positions, two points joined.
  • At a trail junction: place only the lead's cart pointer and WAIT for the next position before connecting the track — the next point shows the branch taken; never guess a branch.
  • Difficulty / build order: route = easy · two points = easy · trails = touchy (junctions, parallel trails). Build: two-point first, then route, trails last once the junction rule is proven.
  • Off-track (Fred 14:12): the track is a PATH each cart is measured against when that cart's own position arrives — not a sweep of every cart on a timer.
  • Fred 10-15 EOD — a trail behind EVERY cart; the lead's trail is one of them. Same builder for every cart, fed by that cart's new fixes on the board.
  • Open: snap distance to a trail; what happens when the lead leaves the trail; (a) a lead change mid-ride — new line (today) or join to the new lead's position; (b) does the trail follow the projected cart during Signal Drop or wait for real fixes; (c) keep OFF_TRACK_MILES as is.
  • Builds: LEADTRACK-1 / TRAILS-1 = the two-point trail rebuilt on the board on each cart's new fix (no tick) + off-track per cart message → T5.5 can then remove the tick · ROUTETRACK (route snap ≤ 50 m) · trails with the junction wait. Each its own build.

C. My cart and my track:

  • My cart = a CoT message from the tablet's GPS (MYCARTMSG 3583eda96, Fred 17:16–17:46): uid MESHTASTIC-!<my radio>, point + altitude, speed/course from the fix; sent on every CHANGED fix (~1 s moving), a keep-alive once per reporting interval parked; through onBoardMessage like every other cart. My radio's own record no longer drives my cart while the tablet has had a fix in the last 15 s. No radio known / group off → the tablet-cart path.
  • My recorded track (GPX) is written from the tablet's own GPS (~1/s). RADIOTRACK (751a0a230) feeds my radio's new fixes to the recorder, in METRES, only for a tablet WITHOUT GPS hardware (the tick passed display feet → 3.3× high altitudes). Not yet tried on a no-GPS tablet.
  • REC active: my cart icon = the head of my track (every fix) (Fred 09:49–09:55) — T5c.
  • No-GPS tablet: my cart's message is built at the radio's frequency from the radio's fix — open (DIAGPOS, written, not applied).
  • Other carts between broadcasts (4c, Fred 07:55–09:39): on the route (≤ 50 m, snapped) a cart keeps moving along the route at its last reported speed until heard again (no cut-off), then ONE correction move to the reported position; off the route it holds at the last report and moves to exactly the reported position (how the group sees someone getting lost). Move only on a NEW fix. Display only — never changes the board. Status still Signal Drop 2 min / Lost 10 min.

D. The lead polyline (T7, Phase 1) and segment display (Phase 2):

  • Phase 1 / T7: the lead transmits his polyline — the last 2 minutes of his track, every broadcast (6 s); receivers mine ONLY its last point as the lead's position. No sender checkbox (v2 change). First check whether the polyline message exists yet; if not, sending it is part of T7.
  • Phase 2 (after certification): polyline SEGMENT display with an on/off toggle on the receiver — the sender always sends the same message. Fred (10-12): would like a 5-minute window, tuned by the last broadcast heard from another cart; count the holes and judge usefulness against the route line alone.
  • Polylines (the lead's own recorded segments) follow the trails work; ROUTETRACK draws the lead path over the route in Phase 1 ("not a stretch").

4. Findings (connection)

  • Two owners reconnect the same radio: Meshtastic's own connect (Android background connect, auto:true) and GroupTrack's recovery (MeshCommLink keep-alive: 20 s not talking / 45 s stuck / 120 s no traffic for a Rider → forced drop + reconnect; 3 tries → banner). Droid 1 13:31–13:35: 7 connects in 4 minutes, then a stuck link.
  • Meshtastic's connect timeout (15 s) does not cancel its pending request (NordicBleInterface.connect() catch: no bleConnection.disconnect()) — requests can stack on the same device. One-line fix found, NOT applied.
  • Meshtastic starts a connect by itself in three places (AndroidRadioInterfaceService): MeshService start, every Bluetooth-state change (including each pairing change — our pairing listener refreshes it), network available.
  • After an install the background connect took 98 s (Droid 2 14:01); a direct connect came up within a second.
  • Recovery only acts after a first connection this session (hConnectedThisSession) — it did not run before the first connect; after a drop it can act while Meshtastic is attaching.
  • Hide Mesh stacks copies of the convoy logic: mesh rail screens remove the ride map from the stack (popUpTo(start){saveState=true}) and Hide Mesh re-runs the gate → a new ride map + view model + tick each time. HIDEMESHBACK v1 failed for that reason; MESHOVERLAY (mesh opens on top of the ride map) written, not applied.
  • Processes 1183 / 1679 seen connecting alongside ours = Android's own Bluetooth service, not another app.
  • Android's last-known position is read in 5 places (my cart fallback in the tick, ride-map entry centring, planning-entry check, planning map, home-state detection). Fred 22:30: it can be a week old, but the first radio / tablet fix moves the map — no change.

5. Decisions (Fred)

  • Rollback method (Fred 20:25): when something breaks, roll back to a known-good point and re-apply one change at a time with a build, a recorded install and the same connect test. Revert commits on 2.7b (version code keeps rising — no downgrade).
  • Rules on RECEIVED PAYLOADS only (Fred 21:53): never on stored data, never during Meshtastic's start-up. One new patch with the end state in mind — no patch on a patch (21:55).
  • GRPCONNECT left out (21:34): connecting works; no connection changes until the recovery design.
  • Connection design (14:30–14:35), for the recovery design: ONE owner. GroupTrack reconnects to the last-used radio and controls failure and reconnect; Meshtastic's own auto-connect turned off; a failed attempt → cancel anything pending → GroupTrack's connect panel; no saved radio → the panel at once. Meshtastic's service does not restart the link after a drop (that is why recovery was built).
  • Recovery rules (Fred 19:59–21:47, design): no GPS from my radio → recovery does not execute; recovery never acts while Meshtastic is attaching; after ~1:45 (3 failed reconnects) → Android mode, nodes cleared, until the radio connection is re-established.
  • T5.2 (22:21–22:30): replicate today: straight to the map, carts positioned as soon as data is available; the cached position on entry is tolerated (the first real fix moves it).
  • T5.4 row 6 (22:21): HOLD silent carts in place.
  • App-wide board (13:08): after the tick is removed. Do not fix what is being replaced (13:04).
  • Hide Mesh (13:45, 19:54): exiting mesh just exits mesh — the ride map is already loaded underneath.
  • Every new-install test records each tablet's log from before the install (11:41) — deploy_v2.sh.

9. Lessons

  • Compare a good startup log with a bad one FIRST (Fred 20:43): we had a recording of every install and still chased radios and configs. Tonight's 20:3x install is the recorded known-good startup.
  • A collector started in init runs at once on the main thread — everything it touches must be declared above init (the 12:14 crash).
  • A crash loop during Meshtastic's connect leaves half-open Bluetooth links — a plausible cause of the afternoon's Bluetooth trouble.
  • Never reason past evidence: every theory today that was not checked against the log was wrong at least in part (second app = Android's own service; "recovery ran before the first connect" — it did not).
  • Our own red message caused damage: it told the rider to Forget while the radio had just started talking.
  • Read the right tablet: with two plugged in every adb command uses -s <serial>.

  pending connect attempt first.
- Recovery design principles proposed (Claude, for Fred's review): one owner of the connection; direct connect after install/
  reconnect; "Connecting..." state instead of plain red; Forget = last resort, never the first advice; no forced cycling of a
  connected link; "not talking" from messages, not a polling loop.

- CORRECTION Fred 14:31: when the Meshtastic attempt fails, GroupTrack takes control by DISCONNECTING ANY PENDING CONNECTION ATTEMPT
  AND OPENING THE GROUPTRACK CONNECT PANEL (the rider picks) -- GroupTrack does not reconnect by itself.
- Fred 14:32: Meshtastic only attempts a connect when a last-used radio is saved. NO saved radio (first install, after Forget,
  after group awareness off) = no Meshtastic attempt -> GroupTrack opens its connect panel straight away.
  FULL RULE: saved radio -> ONE Meshtastic attempt (connect buttons greyed) -> success: done | failure: GroupTrack cancels any
  pending attempt and opens its connect panel. No saved radio -> the connect panel at once.
- DECISION Fred 14:34 (supersedes the Meshtastic-first attempt): TURN MESHTASTIC'S CONNECT CODE OFF. GroupTrack is the ONE owner --
  it reconnects to the last-used radio (GroupTrack holds that data) and controls failure + reconnect; a failed attempt -> cancel
  anything pending -> GroupTrack's connect panel; no saved radio -> the panel at once.
  Levers (code read 14:33): core/ble/BleConnection.kt:83 ConnectionOptions.AutoConnect (background connect = the 98 s) -> Direct
  (one attempt + timeout); Meshtastic's service starts NordicBleInterface.connect() whenever an address is saved (app start,
  install, drop) -> GroupTrack decides; setDeviceAddress("n") = no Meshtastic attempts; MeshCommLink keep-alive forced cycling ->
  removed. To check in the design: does Meshtastic's RadioInterfaceService restart the interface by itself after a drop.
- Fred 14:35: Meshtastic's service does NOT restart the link by itself after a drop -- that is why GroupTrack's auto-recovery was
  built. The extra connect() lines at 13:32:18 / 13:32:53 / 13:35:31 (no GroupTrack reconnect line before them) are therefore most
  likely Android's own re-connect of a link opened with AutoConnect (a background-connect link keeps reconnecting until closed) --
  removed by the same lever: Direct instead of AutoConnect. To confirm in the design.

3. ⚠ The Bluetooth incident (11:48–13:30) — what we know

What happened (11:48 → 13:30): at the 11:48 install (build 1144 = P5.1a + DISCPANEL) Droid 1 never reconnected and Droid 2 showed connected but no radio GPS reached the map; both connection panels showed raw Meshtastic_xxxx names. Every install after it (12:01 clean, 12:21 P5.2, 13:21 the 10:06 code) inherited the problem until the tablets were rebooted (~13:30). Forgetting / re-pairing / Bluetooth off-on got single radios back for a while.

TimeEvent
10:06Droid 2 (last night's P4.4 build): one silent radio, fixed by one automatic reconnect
10:40 · 10:56 · 11:15Installs GRPMODE-1, GRPMODE-2, P5.1a — all came up clean

Ruled out by test: the P5.1a code · the DISCPANEL code (the exact 11:48 build ran clean on rebooted tablets) · a plain install on clean tablets · the radio name table (pulled with adb — intact: !23dec46a "fred&June 2" bt C9:12:23:DE:C4:6A; !0ba3deda "Billy beans" bt DB:92:0B:A3:DE:DA) · an address mismatch (every reconnect went to the right address) · the heartbeat (keys on the node number, not names) · the GroupTrack tick writing to the radio or node database (it never does). Cured by: a tablet reboot (the radios were not reset).

Leading explanation (not proven): Android's Bluetooth on both tablets was in a stuck state — about a week without a reboot and many installs, several within 90 minutes today; the 11:48 install exposed it and every later install inherited it. A registration-slot measurement (dumpsys bluetooth_manager | grep -c grouptrack) read 3 before and 3 after one install with a force-close first — no leak shown; the comparison without a force-close was not run.

Found along the way: (1) after ANY update, Meshtastic's BootCompleteReceiver (MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED) restarts the radio service in the background and reconnects blindly — no GroupTrack startup gate, housekeeping or Bluetooth check runs until the rider opens the app (Fred's observation: "they just started the app"); the same for Play updates. (2) The release-notes popup only shows on a version-NAME change — every test build is 2.7.14, so it never shows on test installs. (3) The name-load-order bug (fixed: NAMELOAD).

4. Decisions (Fred)

  • GRPMODE (G answers): G1 the question as worded · G2 off = GRP Awareness, HUD GROUP and the GPS button greyed; tapping shows the popup group mode needs to be activated in Settings; do not activate unless your mesh device is available · G5 switch Android-only ↔ group aware any time in Settings; off = no radio calls to the comm module.
  • D1–D4 (P5): D1 Member + heading + battery, SNR dropped · D2 shadow first · D3 (Fred): the rules are DISPLAY rules, not retrieval — they stay GroupTrack's; at the tick replacement they are isolated into one GroupTrack unit applied to every message (the tick becomes message-driven) · D4 my own cart is always included; other carts only when group awareness is on; my node keeps the tablet's fix as a fallback.
  • Settings inventory: removed Multicolor, the all-carts track (Lead Cart Only), the KML/GPX switch (always GPX), Removed Carts (Select Carts has it); Show lead track kept as a switch for the lead polyline.
  • Tick replacement design (additions today): the lead's track conforms to the route — a point within 50 m of the displayed route is placed on the route and connected to the last point along the route line; otherwise the received polyline segment is drawn as is (gaps accepted). The lead always sends a polyline; its last point is the current position. The lead's track is erased behind my cart.
  • ⛔ RULE (Fred 12:02): simple additive changes only — NO changes to errors, error processing, recovery or connecting until a complete new end-to-end process is designed. P5.3 (sleep / wake, which touches connecting) goes as its own isolated build.
  • DISCPANEL — parked (Fred: "we cannot put the whole patch"), although tested clean: the GRP box flashes red only when automatic recovery has failed during a recording (the FORGET / PAIR banner's rule: 3 reconnects, still not talking) — not on every drop (v1 rejected); Select Cart says CART ROLES · N carts · radio temporarily disconnected (amber) and you · Android only; recovery-stage text rejected (the banner already says what to do).
  • AUTO-REPAIR — parked for the end-to-end design: after 3 failed reconnects, SCAN first; radio not seen = off / out of range → do not forget; seen but will not connect = the stuck-pairing case → forget + start pairing (rider answers the PIN). Ask Sean what DirtOS does.
  • Test procedure (today): force-close the app before a deploy; when Bluetooth misbehaves, reboot the tablets before judging a build; change ONE thing at a time.

7. Lessons

  • Change ONE thing at a time. Rolling back the code AND rebooting together hid which one cured it; it took a bisect to separate them.
  • Contaminated devices prove nothing. Once Bluetooth was stuck, every later install failed whatever its code — re-establish a clean baseline (reboot) before testing a suspect. Claude wrongly "cleared" DISCPANEL at 12:09 from a contaminated test; Fred caught it.
  • Android refuses a lower version code silently in deploy.sh. To test older code without uninstalling (which wipes maps, rides and settings): a test branch with git revert (version code goes UP), then git merge -s ours back so the main branch stays above it.
  • Git Bash rewrites /sdcard/… into a Windows path — use //sdcard/… with adb shell. git log opens a pager — use git --no-pager log (or press q).
  • Kotlin extension functions need an import (animateFloat) — a fully-qualified call does not reach them.
  • Patch anchors with escapes: the file writer turned \u00b7 into a literal ·; the patch aborted cleanly. Check written patches for escaped characters.
  • Self-tripping post-checks, third time (REPORTNOW's comment named the very token it forbade) — word marker comments to avoid forbidden tokens.

0. FIRST TOMORROW — GRPMODE: "Meshtastic group awareness" or "Android device only" (Fred 10-12 19:49)

Fred: at install, ask: will you use a Meshtastic network for group awareness, or Android device only mode (no group awareness)? It can be changed in Settings. Android-only hides the group awareness functions; they can be turned on at any point in time from the gear / Settings panel on the ride map. Part of tomorrow's project — P5 uses it when the tick is replaced.

What exists today (found 10-12): no such mode. The first-start question is RADIOSETUPSTART (10-01, RadioSetupPrompt.kt: Do you have a mesh radio to use with GroupTrack? YES → GRP Awareness, NO → remembered, set up later); answers kept in grouptrack_radio_setup (no / done). GRP Awareness opens from ConvoyScreen ~1951, ConvoyMapViewerScreen ~2065, the check-in (ConvoyCheckIn ~185) and the startup gate (ConvoyAuthorityGateScreenV2 ~899). This is the old "Group riding switch / GRP Awareness switch" from the 10-06 / 10-07 2.7b list, now made concrete.

PieceProposed
The settingOne GroupTrack setting, groupAwareness = MESH or ANDROID_ONLY, stored with GroupTrack's settings (ConvoySettingsRepository). GroupTrack's choice, not the comm side's.
The install questionReplaces RADIOSETUPSTART's question with the two choices. MESH → today's YES path (GRP Awareness: connect, apply the default). ANDROID_ONLY → nothing radio-related asked again.
Upgrading ridersNot asked again: saved answer no → ANDROID_ONLY; done or any saved radio config → MESH; nothing saved → asked.
Hidden in ANDROID_ONLYThe GRP Awareness box (both maps) and its panel; the GPS "sent every N s" button; Apply ride to radio and Saved configs entries; the check-in's radio setup step (check-in still records role and track); the power-cycle / FORGET banner; the radio network choice on the ride form stays (a ride may be shared with radio users).
The comm side when offNo reconnects, no scanning, no keep-alive tries, no FORGET banner — additive CommLink.setActive(Boolean) (off = drop the link and hold the keep-alive, as with Bluetooth off). On → the saved radio is connected again.
The tick (ties to P5)ANDROID_ONLY = the tablet is the only node (today's Android-only path, phone GPS); members() is never asked. MESH = P5.1's members() path.
The switchGear / Settings panel on the ride map: "Group awareness with a Meshtastic radio" on/off. Turning it on opens GRP Awareness.
⚠ FALLBACK RULE (Fred 10-12 19:53) — do not confuse the two: Android device only is a MODE the rider chooses (no radio asked, group functions hidden). The fallback is different: in Meshtastic group-awareness mode, when the radio disconnects, Android data is substituted for radio data on the local node — my cart takes the tablet's GPS so its information stays current (today's LINKFIX / LINKFIX2: other carts held at their last tick position, nothing invented). When the radio returns, my cart goes back to the radio's data. The data must roll back and forth with the link, as many times as it drops, with the group functions staying visible. GRPMODE must not switch the mode when the link drops, and P5.1 must keep this substitution when the tick's source moves to members() (with the radio down, members() is stale or empty — my cart comes from the tablet). Tablet check for both: in Meshtastic mode, radio off → my cart keeps moving on the tablet's GPS, others held, GRP Awareness red; radio on → my cart back on the radio's position, same cart id; repeat twice.
⚠ MY-CART SOURCE RULE (Fred 10-12 19:55) — refines the fallback rule: with GRP Awareness on, my cart is ONE cart, keyed by my callsign. Its data comes from the radio when the radio's own report has been refreshed within the last 2 broadcast intervals; if the radio's data is more than 2 broadcast intervals old, from the tablet. A fresh radio report switches it back. Same cart, same callsign, either way. Today the switch follows link health (radio "not talking" = no own report for 45 s, LINKHB). To settle 10-13 (G5): which interval — proposed: 2 × the moving interval while the tablet's GPS shows motion (e.g. 6 s → 12 s), 2 × the stationary 30 s when stopped (60 s), so a parked cart does not flip between its 30-s reports. The intervals come from the comm side (currentReporting(), P5.2) — so P5.2 moves before P5.1b.
✅ G5 DECIDED (Fred 10-12 19:57) — "really simple": the limit is 2 broadcast intervals (moving every 6 s → 12 s). When my radio's last report is older than that, my cart takes the tablet's GPS only if the tablet has moved since the last update; if it has not moved, my cart stays where the last update put it. No motion test on the radio side, no separate stationary limit — a parked cart simply does not change. Fresh radio report → back to the radio. Same cart, same callsign.
✅ TWO POSITIONS IN MY CART'S RECORD (Fred 10-12 19:58): my node records both the radio's GPS (position + fix time, from the comm side) and the tablet's GPS (position + fix time). The display applies the G5 rule to choose which to show: radio fix ≤ 2 broadcast intervals old (12 s at 6 s) → radio; older AND the tablet has moved since the last update → tablet; otherwise the last shown position. Why: it ends the GPS jitter between cart and radio — today one position field is overwritten by whichever source wrote last, so two receivers a few feet apart make the cart hop. Both values stay in the record for logging and tests. Only my own cart has two sources; other carts are radio-only and unchanged. Lands in P5.1b (ConvoyNode gains the radio and tablet fixes for my cart; the choice is one function, logged when it switches).
✅ SCOPE SPLIT (Fred 10-12 20:01): P5 redefines and populates the node elements; the display rules wait for the tick replacement.
WhenWhat
P5 (P5.1b)ConvoyNode redefined: radio fix (lat, lon, fix time — from members()) and tablet fix (lat, lon, fix time — the tablet's GPS) as separate elements, populated every tick. My cart: both. Other carts: radio fix only. Android device only: tablet fix only, radio fix empty. The display does not change in P5 — today's LINKFIX behaviour stays; the record just carries more. P5 stays plumbing, nothing visible.
Tick replacement (later)The display rules: radio fix ≤ 2 broadcast intervals (12 s at 6 s) → radio; older and the tablet has moved since the last update → tablet; otherwise the last position. Android device only → always the tablet fix (Fred 19:59: "if no group awareness we just use the android, which is the second value").

Decisions for Fred (tomorrow, before code): G1 the exact question wording; G2 the hidden list above (anything to keep visible?); G3 can it be switched mid-ride (proposed: yes, but only when no ride is checked in — or it ends the check-in first); G4 the comm side off = radio disconnected (proposed yes, saved radio kept for when it comes back on).

Order (proposed): GRPMODE first (≈ ½ day: setting + question + hide + setActive), then P5.1a/b — so the tick's source choice is made once, by this setting.

📍 TICK REPLACEMENT — the plan agreed 10-12 evening (Fred; not P5, later in 2.7b)

Method (Fred): segmented — develop, implement, then evaluate performance in the field. "Plenty of time to figure this out."

PartWhat
1. Cart positions (first)Every cart a dot at its current position; my cart leaves its own track behind my pin. Display rules from P5's node elements: my cart radio fix ≤ 2 broadcast intervals (12 s at 6 s) → radio; older and the tablet has moved since the last update → tablet; else the last position. Android device only → always the tablet fix. Other carts: radio only (Android-extended node data is my cart only).
2. Lead polyline experiment (once 1 works)The lead broadcasts every 6 s with the last 2 minutes of its track; receivers plot the lead path over the route; count the holes and judge usefulness against the route line alone. Changes the 10-06 option A: the lead sends the polyline, not separate GPS (its last point is the current position). Fred: would like to reach a 5-minute window, with the window tuned by the last broadcast heard from another cart (1 min floor, 5 min cap; older part at coarser detail so 5 min fits one packet). Fred is wary of duplicating the lead's traffic — measure airtime (channel use is already shown in the GPS panel).
Lost commIf the lead hears no cart, it has lost comm with the group → a clear warning on the lead's tablet (same check for any rider). Time limit to decide.
Route GPX in the inviteThe ride invite carries a second file, <ride>.gpx (route as a <trk> + trailhead <wpt>) so non-GroupTrack riders (onX, Gaia, Garmin, CalTopo) can import the raw route. Today ConvoyRideSend sends only the .convoy file; planner routes already export GPX (buildRouteGpxById, as <rte>). The invite text must say which file is which — GroupTrack riders tap the .convoy (GroupTrack also opens .gpx, which would import the route only). Part of the tick-replacement release (Fred).

Reference numbers (10-12): one Meshtastic packet ≈ 230 bytes of data ≈ 40–50 points (first point ~8 bytes, then ~4 bytes each). Simplified (Douglas–Peucker, ~5 m) riding needs ~3–10 points per minute → 1 min ≈ 20–50 bytes, 2 min ≈ 35–90 bytes, 5 min ≈ 70–210 bytes (twisty may need coarser older detail). 20 min does not fit one packet and is not needed — each tablet stitches the full lead track from the pieces.

Why the ride must reach every rider (Fred): a point on a map without a route is useless. Group tracking needs (1) faithful distribution of ride invites to all GroupTrack users before the ride and (2) compliance — even Android-only riders stay on course far better with the ride imported. AWS invite tracking is a while away, so the user docs carry the message now (manual v55, Quick Start v82, release notes v35: "Before every ride: send the ride invite to every rider"). Later: check-in offers to import a ride that is not on the tablet; tablet-to-tablet ride passing at the trailhead.

Open, separate: the recorded track is losing GPS during rides — diagnose first (gaps vs screen-off/sleep times → Android throttling; random gaps in steep terrain → sky); fill along the known trail where it cannot be fixed at source.

5. Lessons

  • A patch's own comment can trip its own post-check — twice now (P2b "scanModel", P4.4 "radioOps()"). Word marker comments so they never contain a forbidden token; the scratch test caught it before Fred saw it.
  • gt_build's "N UNCOMMITTED change(s)" counts tracked files only — new untracked files are in the build but not in the count; a move needs an explicit git add of the folders (git then records renames).
  • Long commit messages: plain ASCII only (Fred's paste picked up bad characters); give add / commit / push as three separate commands and always paste git log --oneline -1.
  • "Never applied" ≠ "never set up": GRP Awareness judged the radio by this tablet's records; the radio's own network is the truth (RADIONET).
  • A stale report: the comm side's applyReport outlives the screen — cleared at the start of every apply (found in scratch review before sending P4.2).

6. Other threads today

  • Sean Connell's meshtastic_flutter (github.com/seancmt/meshtastic_flutter, BSD-3): a fork of Madhav Gupta's package (0.0.3) + 12 commits by Sean (9–10 Apr 2026): write-with-response, non-zero want_config_id, iOS MTU / Android-12 permission guards, sendData, sendAdminConfig. ~650 hand-written lines + generated protobufs. No reconnect, sleep or timeout handling, no pairing, no multi-step config write. Nothing to take for Android; a possible starting point for the 2.9 Apple Bluetooth layer behind the same comm contract. Fred's reply to Sean: reconnects, sleeps and Bluetooth timeouts were his biggest T1000-E problems and where he wrote his own recovery code.
  • AI cost question: GroupTrack makes no AI calls; the trail narrative (RouteExplorer.narrative()) is template text built on the tablet from OSM features — free per rider, works offline. If richer AI text is ever wanted: generate once per trail on the AWS server and store it.

📚 10-11 (evening) — USER DOCS NOW IN THE DAILY EOD SET · manual v53 · release notes v33 · Quick Start v80

The rule (Fred 10-11): the current manual, release notes and Quick Start are carried in the EOD set every day, beside the handoff, living master, WHERE WE ARE and ledger. Each day's user-visible changes go into them that day — text updated, and every screen that changed labelled in the manual as it changes. That is the only way to stay on top of the changes; a catch-up later becomes a large task that misses them.

  • Labels in the manual (visible, with a grep-able RECAPTURE-2026-10-11 comment): 📷 NEW IMAGE NEEDED (red — the picture is out of date) · 📷 IMAGE TO CHECK (amber — may have changed) · 📷 NEW SCREEN — CAPTURE TO ADD (blue — a screen not yet pictured). A label is removed only when its picture is recaptured. Labels are visible to readers until then — remove or hide them before a public refresh only if Fred decides so.
  • Each EOD: for every rider-visible change of the day (a release, or a build headed to one): release notes entry · manual text · Quick Start step if a step changed · a label on every affected picture. New version numbers each day something changed; never reuse a file name.
  • Online copy (grouptrack.org) is refreshed at release time from the current versions (standing step, WEBDOCS-2026-10-03).

10-10 — ROOT CAUSE 1: the tester's missing trails

  • Symptom: after Remove all trail data + Utah re-import, the tester (and then Droid 2, deliberately) had no trails at all — e.g. Four Mile Wash, way 1003674700. The PC trace (gt_osmtrace_2026-10-10_v2.py) said the trail is KEPT and SHOWN, hash identical, land PUBLIC — so the import was right; the device's step 9 was not.
  • Mechanism: step 9 (OwnershipReclass.run) applied category, use AND land in one pass, and was skipped when the ownership file was missing or refused. Skipped → carto_code NULL → the map's filter carto_code NOT IN (off list) is NULL for NULL → every unclassified trail hidden, whatever Map Keys says.
  • Why the file was refused: OOMGUARD (09-15) checked heap headroom against the file size before reading. The streaming reader (09-17) made it obsolete, but it stayed. Fred's own 72 MB file (09-12) was loaded before the guard existed; a fresh download is 78.7 MB and was refused every time. The step reported "0", nothing in the log said why.
  • Fred's rule (10-10): "our categories are meaningless unless they are applied" — "the process has to run". Missing input → a default (land PUBLIC), never a skipped step.
  • Proof: failure replicated on Droid 2 first (clear → Utah import → no trails), the refusal found in Droid 1's log, release k on both tablets → all trails back; the launch pass repairs a tablet without a re-import.

10-10 — ROOT CAUSE 2: "Cannot use this target: String arg == null" on check-in

  • Symptom: "Cannot use this target: String arg == null" right after group 1b on every CHECK IN apply that restarts the radio, any ride; the radio nevertheless ended exactly on target (Fred's compare). Applying from "Apply ride to radio" never showed it; re-applying a ride already on the radio (nothing to write, no restart) never showed it.
  • Values were never the issue: the configurator reads only the ride's network.mesh (region, preset, slot, hop limit, TX power, role, channel name, key), the default asset's standalone reporting group and the callsign — all required; the two rides differed only in channel name, hop limit and key.
  • Mechanism: group 1b restarts the radio → node id goes null and back → LaunchedEffect(nodeId) rebuilds targets → the CHECK IN auto-apply LaunchedEffect(targets) fires again while autoRideId is still set → choose() runs mid-apply against a half-reconnected radio and paints the error over a successful apply.
  • Fix: APPLYGUARD (above). Tablet-tested 10-10: no errors on check-in.

Network choices — what Fred asked for (10-10, verbatim intent): "the Organization network replaced by GroupTrack Default, the Creator's network shown as Creator's name – Network id, and then private network." A ride on the GroupTrack default is heard by every GroupTrack radio — accepted by Fred. "No scheduled ride" check-in also uses the default unless the rider asks to keep the radio as it is.

Release l tablet tests (all passed 10-10, Fred): (1) Create Ride shows the three choices; (2) check in to a default ride sets the radio to GroupTrack, re-check-in shows "Radio ready"; (3) no-ride check-in applies the default, "Keep" leaves it; (4) no error on any check-in apply.

Method lessons 10-10 (keep):

  • A step that depends on an input must run with a default when the input is missing — never skip silently (Fred: "the process has to run").
  • Remove a guard when its reason goes: OOMGUARD outlived the reader it protected and broke every fresh import for three weeks.
  • Replicate the failure before fixing it (Fred cleared Droid 2 and reproduced the tester's empty map first).
  • Compose: an automatic action inside a LaunchedEffect keyed on state that changes during a long operation will re-fire — gate it on the phase.
  • Compare the paths that work with the path that fails (check-in vs manual apply; restart vs nothing-to-write) — that, not the values, found the apply error.
  • Environment: PC Python SSL "certificate expired" → download with curl; Drive upload looping → router reboot + restart Drive; deploy installed a stale build (29321322) when Drive lagged → deploy by exact name, always; Droid 2 hides app logs → logcat on Droid 1; recommit step −1 moves .db files into the inbox → moved to D:\GroupTrack_archive\db\.
  • My errors (Claude), recorded: said the land default "has no impact" (land was coupled with categories in step 9 — wrong); assumed Fred's 72 MB file had passed the guard (it pre-dated the guard); did not build the network option when first asked; removed the organizer choice when Fred wanted three choices; 10-11: gave a Play "What's new" text as 488 characters when it was 538.

10-09 — FINDINGS (what the "missing trails" actually were):

  • Steamboat (Droid 1, ~13:39–14:00): rider trails WERE in the DB (written 13:38) and the map's query returned them; they did not draw for ~3 hours, then did. Best explanation (fits the timeline, NOT caught in the act): every session began with a clear-and-rebuild that renumbered them, and the map did not reload in that session. Release i removes both causes; TRAILDRAW now records it if it ever recurs.
  • Cedar Mtn w/ Steve and Mike (Kolob): the job DID cover the ride (106 trails under it, 0.01 mi uncovered); TRAILDRAW on the bare screen: sent 231 → page ok 231. The bare sections were Map Keys: forestry switched off AND Land = Public — ~70% of the ride runs on forestry/access roads classified PRIVATE as WHOLE roads (Upper Basin Rd 7.9 mi, Kolob Terrace Rd 5.8, Kolob Rd 8.2, Miners Peak Rd 8.5, Black Mountain Rd 5.5). Toggling Private on → they draw.
  • Map Keys is an EXCLUSION list on trails.carto_code (spatial DB only — the extension copy is never read by the map). Codes the panel does not list can never be switched off and draw in default light blue: UGRC 1 - … to 5 - …, 9 - Link, null; and TERRA (1,745) / SNOW (253) (New England data; extension copy says "shape only").
  • Spatial ↔ extension alignment (Droid 1, 182,512 trails): 0 trails without properties, 0 orphan property rows — the clears/reloads did NOT misalign them; 2,009 category differences = TERRA/SNOW (+ a handful of two-source disagreements).
  • Tester's missing OSM trails: public OSM trails, IN his DB (re-import added none), not drawn. Agreed procedure (Fred): he updates to release i (the quote fix can blank whole screens); if still missing → remove all trail data (both DBs) and re-import his state, then ADD TRAILS FROM TRACKS.
  • Two more page defects seen in grouptrack_map.html (not yet proven to bite): setTileUrl hides trails for any source other than SAT/TOPO (TOPO+ is the default since 10-06; the next pan re-shows them); the label overlay tiles go into the SAME pane as the trail canvas and are re-added after it on every base change (can paint over trails while SVG tracks stay on top). Check with chrome://inspect (the WEBDEBUG build) when it next happens.
